# Query and storage findings

This append-only log records storage behavior discovered while implementing
the [PromQL](PROMQL_FEATURE_MATRIX.md) and
[LogsQL](LOGSQL_FEATURE_MATRIX.md) feature maps. It is not a substitute for a
regression test. Every resolved finding links to the test and the public
documentation that now pins the behavior.

## Baseline constraints

These are known constraints at the 2026-08-04 `a72634e` baseline. They are not
new defects, but query work must not accidentally obscure them.

| ID | behavior | consequence |
|---|---|---|
| `QSF-001` | Metric window kernels use `(T-window,T]`. | This matches PromQL range-vector boundaries, but callers must still implement lookback, staleness, counter extrapolation, and output-label rules. |
| `QSF-002` | `timeless_window(..., 'rate'|'increase')` is a storage kernel, not a complete PromQL evaluator. | The Rust API must apply PromQL reset, extrapolation, carry-in, sparse-series, and name-retention semantics rather than relabeling the native result as PromQL. |
| `QSF-003` | Metric storage contains float samples and timestamps but no native-histogram sample type. | Classic `_bucket` histograms are queryable as float series. Native-histogram-only operators and functions remain deferred until a typed storage design exists. |
| `QSF-004` | Metric chunks and public binary batches preserve exact float bits, but the product has no end-to-end Prometheus stale-marker contract. | Five-minute lookback can be implemented, but exact marker parity also needs marker-capable ingress and query paths that distinguish the stale payload from ordinary NaNs. It must not be inferred from every NaN. |
| `QSF-005` | Logs retain typed nested metadata, while the term index intentionally covers only selected low-cardinality keys. | Arbitrary metadata predicates are correct through bounded decode, but only indexed keys can promise posting-list pruning. Measurements decide whether another key earns indexing. |
| `QSF-006` | Message substring filtering and many metadata transformations require block decode. | The API can implement them correctly now; an extension index or new block metadata is justified only by measured workloads. |
| `QSF-007` | Timeless logs do not currently assign VictoriaLogs-compatible `_stream_id` identities. | Ordinary field filters fit now. `_stream_id`, stream-context, and stream-specific optimizations are deferred pending an explicit stream model. |

## Findings

Add one entry for every unexpected correctness, performance, durability,
memory, or planner behavior found during feature work.

| ID | date | matrix row | observation | expected behavior | evidence/test | disposition | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `QSF-008` | 2026-08-04 | `LQL-F02` | Relative LogsQL time was computed from the process wall clock inside parsing and set no upper bound, so deterministic boundary coverage was impossible and future-dated rows matched. Converting the corrected half-open interval directly also made valid `_time:0s` look like a malformed reversed range. | One request-owned evaluation instant defines upstream's `[now-duration,now)` native-unit window; zero duration is a valid empty bounded query; both reach the same public planner used by the HTTP route. | pinned `LQL-F02-relative-window` and `LQL-F02-zero-duration-is-empty`; `relative_time_uses_the_injected_query_clock_exactly`; `session_ten_relative_logsql_pins_inclusive_lower_exclusive_upper_and_reopens` | Extracted a reusable Rust LogsQL planner, injected one query clock, set `ts_min=now-duration` and the inclusive storage bound to `ts_max=now-1 native unit`, and represent `0s` with impossible inclusive bounds that return no rows without unbounded work. Both microsecond edges and the empty case pass before flush and after reopen. Storage formats, batching, indexes, compression, and extension contracts are unchanged. | resolved |
| `QSF-009` | 2026-08-04 | `LQL-F06` | The exact `error` baseline prunes to one error-family block but decodes 4,096 entries to return 1,024 because `error`, `critical`, `alert`, and `emergency` share a physical partition. | Exact severity fidelity is preserved even when the coarse partition needs decode. | `docs/evidence/2026-08-04_query_baseline.json`; `http_uses_the_established_8192_entry_buffer_without_request_flushes` | Accepted storage/write tradeoff. Consider finer severity metadata only after representative workloads prove a material read benefit without harming 8,192-entry batching/compression. | accepted |
| `QSF-010` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S06` | The public raw TVF uses inclusive read bounds, while a PromQL range selector uses `(T-W,T]`. | The API and direct SQL recipe must discard samples exactly on the left boundary without changing the extension contract. | `tests/cli.sh` section 33; `session_four_pins_promql_selector_window_errors_and_reopen` | Keep the general raw contract intact and apply the one-boundary predicate in SQL/Rust. No new extension primitive or format change is justified. | accepted |
| `QSF-011` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-R01` | The existing Rust `avg_over_time` response retained `__name__`, despite the pinned Prometheus fixture already requiring range-function metric-name removal. | Range-function output labels omit the metric name. | `tests/query_oracles/prometheus/promql_smoke.yml`; `session_four_pins_promql_selector_window_errors_and_reopen` | Corrected both packed-window and raw fallback response paths and pinned the exact label set. | resolved |
| `QSF-012` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S06` | Metrics API accounting exposed returned frame/response bytes but the public extension stats had no candidate-chunk, payload-byte, or decode counters for packed raw reads. | Direct SQLite/libSQL users and API benchmarks can attribute selected series, candidate chunks, stored payload bytes, fully decoded points, buffered points considered, and returned points. | `tests/cli.sh` section 34; `docs/evidence/2026-08-04_session2_pql_s06.json` | Added cumulative, per-process `raw_batch_query_*` counters to `timeless_stats`; storage and frame formats are unchanged. | resolved |
| `QSF-013` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S06` | An incremental local release build retained the previous Git SHA because the common build script watched only an environment override, not the active Git ref. | Checked evidence must identify the exact source commit that produced every measured signal binary. | `test_evidence_rejects_a_stale_release_binary_identity`; Session 2 evidence harness | The build script now watches `HEAD`, its symbolic ref, and `packed-refs`; the evidence harness fails before workload setup when a binary SHA differs from `HEAD`. Release CI's explicit SHA override remains authoritative. | resolved |
| `QSF-014` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S11`, `PQL-S01`, `PQL-R01` | The Rust response formatter appended `.0` to integral float samples, while Prometheus emits the shortest value string (`"10"`, not `"10.0"`). | Scalar, vector, matrix, and range-function values share Prometheus's shortest finite formatting plus exact `NaN`/`+Inf`/`-Inf` spellings. | `tests/query_oracles/prometheus/api_cases.json`; `session_two_promql_scalar_literals_match_prometheus`; `session_four_pins_promql_selector_window_errors_and_reopen` | Corrected the shared formatter and updated every existing exact response regression. Stored float bits and SQLite formats are unchanged. | resolved |
| `QSF-015` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S12` | PromQL has millisecond durations and evaluation timestamps, while the retained metric storage and packed window TVF use integer seconds. Whole-second windows retain the packed window path; any subsecond temporal component falls back to the public packed raw path and post-filters exact boundaries. No extension or format change was justified. | resolved |
| `QSF-016` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S12`, `PQL-S19` | Invalid Prometheus `time` and `step` inputs silently fell back to defaults, broadening a malformed request into a valid query. | Invalid temporal syntax fails as `bad_data`; omitted parameters alone receive defaults. | `prometheus_time_and_duration_inputs_use_a_millisecond_clock`; `session_two_promql_duration_literals_preserve_milliseconds` | Replaced permissive parsing with explicit errors and the pinned duration parser. | `QSF-017` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S16` | Prometheus accepts `lookback_delta=0` and interprets it as the configured five-minute default, not as an empty or exact-timestamp window. | Request-scoped lookback must preserve the upstream zero/default distinction and open-left boundary. | `PQL-S16-open-lookback-boundary`; `PQL-S16-zero-selects-default-lookback`; `session_two_promql_grid_and_lookback_match_prometheus`; `docs/evidence/2026-08-04_session2_pql_s16.json` | Added a pinned real-Prometheus remote-write oracle and apply the resolved lookback to every lowered selector. | resolved |
| `QSF-018` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S12`, `PQL-S16`, `PQL-S19` | Passing a negative or non-finite numeric duration into the upstream parser utility can panic while constructing `std::time::Duration`; the API previously called it directly for `step`. | Malformed public input must return `bad_data` and never unwind the server. | `prometheus_time_and_duration_inputs_use_a_millisecond_clock`; metrics API unit suite | Numeric duration validation now rejects negative/non-finite/range-invalid values before calling the pinned grammar parser. | resolved |
| `QSF-019` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S16`, `PQL-S20` | The range-grid size check used saturating `i64` subtraction followed by unchecked `+ 1`; extreme valid timestamps could wrap in release builds and bypass the 11,000-point gate. | Grid cardinality calculation is exact over the complete accepted timestamp domain. | `prometheus_grid_size_rejects_extreme_ranges_without_overflow`; metrics API unit suite | Grid size is calculated in `i128` before applying the limit. | `QSF-021` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S19` | The Rust range endpoint defaulted omitted `start`, `end`, and `step`, while Prometheus requires each parameter and reports which one is missing. | Canonical Prometheus range requests fail before planning or storage reads when a required parameter is absent. | pinned `PQL-S19-*` API cases; `session_two_promql_errors_match_the_documented_contract`; `docs/evidence/2026-08-04_session2_pql_s19.json` | Prometheus request parsing now requires all three fields and emits parameter-scoped `bad_data`; native Timeless range parameters retain their separate defaults. | resolved |
| `QSF-022` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S19`, `PQL-S20` | Prometheus 3.13.2 ignores unknown query parameters, accepts non-finite `time`, and permits 11,000 intervals (11,001 points). | `QSF-023` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S19` | The unprefixed `/api/v1/query` routes multiplex the pre-existing native Timeless API based on the presence of `query`; therefore an omitted `query` cannot have the same error as the canonical Prometheus route without breaking native clients. | Prometheus compatibility has an unambiguous canonical route while retained native behavior stays reachable. | `session_two_promql_errors_match_the_documented_contract`; server route tests | `/prometheus/api/v1/query*` is canonical for exact request parsing. Unprefixed routes share PromQL behavior only when `query` is present; this exception is documented rather than hidden. | accepted |
| `QSF-024` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S20` | Final HTTP row/byte limits could reject an oversized result only after `timeless_raw_frame` or `timeless_window_batches` had read, decoded, and materialized every candidate point. | Resource limits bound storage decode and packed-result allocation before work is performed, including for direct SQLite/libSQL callers. | `write_flush_query_recover`; `tests/cli.sh` sections 22 and 34; `session_two_promql_limits_bound_grid_work_results_response_and_deadline` | Added optional, capability-advertised `max_work_points` hidden inputs. Existing arities and storage formats remain compatible; the Rust API requires the advertised guard for PromQL and applies result/response/deadline limits while evaluating. | resolved |
| `QSF-025` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S20` | After a Git commit that changed no extension source file, Cargo reused `libtimeless_ext.so` with the previous commit embedded in `timeless_capabilities()`, even though the signal binaries rebuilt with the current identity. | Every measured server and extension artifact identifies the exact checked evidence source. | `test_evidence_rejects_a_stale_release_extension_identity`; query evidence preflight | Made the extension build script watch Git HEAD, its symbolic ref, and packed refs just like the server build; evidence now rejects a stale extension before workload setup. | resolved |
| `QSF-026` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S04` | Public packed metric reads intentionally take one exact metric name; a PromQL nameless selector can span many names. | Catalog-prune names and series before payload decode, then compose bounded exact-name reads without teaching the extension PromQL syntax. | `session_three_promql_nameless_selectors_expand_before_reads_and_reopen`; pinned Prometheus smoke cases | Reused `timeless_series` for no-decode expansion and `timeless_raw_frame` for each selected name. | `QSF-027` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S04` | The first correct nameless plan queried `timeless_series` once per metric name, costing 2.883 ms p50 and 3.145 ms p95 to return one series from a 576-series/65-name fixture. | Multi-name planning should cross the public catalog once and remain bounded even when few or no series match. | `docs/evidence/2026-08-04_session3_pql_s04.json` before/final measurements; `session_three_promql_nameless_selectors_expand_before_reads_and_reopen` | Stream the complete public catalog once, check cancellation per row, cap considered series and retained label bytes, group matching rows by metric, then issue exact-name payload reads. No shadow-table access or extension change. | resolved |
| `QSF-028` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S07`, `PQL-S08` | `timeless_grid` and `timeless_window*` correctly emit their lookup-grid timestamps, while PromQL `offset`/`@` must emit the outer evaluation timestamp after selecting at a different time. | Never present a shifted native-kernel timestamp as modified PromQL output. | `tests/cli.sh` section 45; `session_three_promql_temporal_modifiers_preserve_selection_and_output_time`; pinned `PQL-S07`/`PQL-S08` API cases | Temporal modifiers use bounded public raw frames and apply exact lookup boundaries while writing outer timestamps in Rust. The executable SQL recipe shifts or cross-joins the outer grid explicitly. No extension change is justified. | accepted |
| `QSF-029` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S09` | A root subquery can reuse the bounded streaming matrix response, but a parent range function needs random/rolling access to those inner points; globally refactoring selectors into an owned value graph would add that memory cost to every query. | Bound and expose only the intermediate materialization required by a parent AST node while preserving streaming selector hot paths. | `session_three_promql_subqueries_align_bound_cancel_and_reopen`; `session_four_cancels_dropped_promql_requests_and_reuses_the_reader`; `docs/evidence/2026-08-04_session3_pql_s09.json` | Keep the general extension unchanged. The 512-series consuming shape materialized 16,384 points and raised whole-process HWM by a measured 2,988 KiB (8.96%) over the preceding identical-fixture run. | accepted |
| `QSF-030` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-S09` | Adding recursive plan variants grew the by-value reader-channel command from a small control message to at least 240 bytes, tripping the strict production `large_enum_variant` gate. | Query grammar growth must not linearly inflate every queued reader command or reader-channel slot. | `reader_queue_commands_keep_recursive_query_plans_behind_indirection`; production all-target clippy gate | Box the owned `ReadRequest` once at the reader-queue boundary and keep retry/query semantics unchanged. The queue command is pinned at no more than 64 bytes; AST/storage contracts are unchanged. | resolved |
| `QSF-031` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-O01` | Prometheus 3.13.2 removes `__name__` from a vector under unary minus, including after double negation; treating negation as a value-only map would retain a wrong metric identity. | Unary composition preserves non-name labels and timestamps but removes the metric name at every unary node. | pinned `PQL-O01-unary-vector-drops-name` API case; `session_four_promql_unary_minus_preserves_types_labels_limits_and_reopen` | Apply name removal in the Rust AST evaluator, keep the public extension/grid contract unchanged, and document SQL as the numeric storage foundation rather than a complete PromQL envelope. | resolved |
| `QSF-032` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-O01` | A reusable Rust AST value bridge decodes and re-encodes child vectors, adding host work even though unary minus does not require another storage read. | Composition must stay bounded without moving language syntax into the extension or imposing owned-value allocation on streaming selector/window hot paths. | `docs/evidence/2026-08-04_session4_pql_o01.json`; intermediate counter and limit regression | Accept the measured bounded cost: versus the same-run 512-series × four-point selector grid, unary adds 1.272 ms p50 and 1.355 ms p95 while storage counters remain identical. | `QSF-033` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-O02` | The root underscored-number compatibility shim captured `20 - metric_name` because the complete expression began numerically and contained an underscore later in the metric identifier. | Root-literal compatibility must never preempt a valid compound AST expression. | `root_underscored_number_shim_does_not_capture_binary_metric_names`; scalar-left real-extension arithmetic regression | Restrict the shim to input composed entirely of numeric-literal characters before validating underscores; compound input returns to the pinned PromQL parser. | `QSF-034` | 2026-08-04 | `PQL-O02`, `PQL-O05` | General vector/vector arithmetic must evaluate two independently selectable child expressions; the 512-series × four-point fixture therefore performs 1,024 candidate-chunk reads and decodes 32,768 stored points per query. | Keep each operand bounded and read exactly once, then match in Rust per evaluation timestamp without moving PromQL or private storage access into the extension. | `docs/evidence/2026-08-04_session4_pql_o02_o05.json`; real-extension cumulative-work/cardinality regressions | Accepted: 2,048 results measure 8.895/9.696/10.054 ms p50/p95/p99 with exactly 4,096 intermediate points.
